Display Hub: third-party notices
================================

Display Hub's own code is under the MIT licence, in the LICENSE file beside this one. Display Hub
also uses work by other people, under their own licences. This file lists that work, with the
licence texts and copyright notices those licences ask to be passed on.

Every download carries this same file, so not everything in it is in every download:

  The desktop app and the tools (Windows, macOS and Linux) hold the parts in 1, 2 and 3.
  The Raspberry Pi download (hubd and hub-send) holds only some of the crates in 3.

  1. The Geist font
  2. The official display firmware
  3. Rust crates

Display Hub's source code: https://github.com/penguin97040/display-hub

================================================================================================
1. The Geist font
================================================================================================

The desktop app draws its text in Geist and Geist Mono, and the display firmware in Geist, from
https://github.com/vercel/geist-font. The font files are used unchanged. Their licence, the SIL
Open Font License 1.1, is below. Montserrat and Font Awesome, in part 2, are under it too.

Copyright 2026 The Geist Project Authors (https://github.com/vercel/geist-font)

This Font Software is licensed under the SIL Open Font License, Version 1.1.
This license is copied below, and is also available with a FAQ at:
https://openfontlicense.org

The goals of the Open Font License (OFL) are to stimulate worldwide

================================================================================================
2. The official display firmware
================================================================================================

The app downloads include the official firmware for the Waveshare ESP32-S3-Touch-AMOLED-1.8
(V2) display: the .dhfw file in the firmware folder. It holds the firmware, its bootloader and its
partition table, built with Espressif's ESP-IDF v5.5.6 and the components below. Display Hub's own
code in it is under the MIT licence. Only the parts with code in the firmware are listed, as the
linker's record of a release build (its map file) shows them.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2.1 ESP-IDF, Espressif's framework

Source: https://github.com/espressif/esp-idf, version v5.5.6
(commit 880a203ae603d08a94d06aa01e0d6761958e5e9e).

Copyright (C) 2015-2026 Espressif Systems (Shanghai) CO LTD. Licensed under the Apache License 2.0
(its text is in 2.4).

This covers ESP-IDF's own code in the firmware and in its bootloader: start-up, drivers, flash,
storage (NVS), the HTTP client, TLS glue, networking glue and the C library glue. It also covers
Espressif's Wi-Fi and radio (PHY) libraries, which ESP-IDF supplies as object code
(https://github.com/espressif/esp32-wifi-lib and https://github.com/espressif/esp-phy-lib, under
the same Apache License 2.0).

The parts of ESP-IDF that come from other projects, and are in this firmware, are in 2.2.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

2.2.11 GCC's runtime libraries

libgcc and libstdc++, from the GCC compiler (https://gcc.gnu.org), are under the GNU General Public
License version 3 with the GCC Runtime Library Exception. The exception lets a program compiled
with GCC be distributed under its own terms, so no notice is required. They are named here for
completeness.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2.3 Components from the ESP Component Registry

Each is pinned to one exact version in the firmware's main/idf_component.yml, and
firmware/dependencies.lock records its hash. firmware/THIRD_PARTY.md has more about each.

2.3.1 Espressif's and Waveshare's components (Apache License 2.0)

Each ships the unchanged Apache License 2.0, whose text is in 2.4.

  waveshare/esp32_s3_touch_amoled_1_8 2.0.3, the board package
      https://github.com/waveshareteam/Waveshare-ESP32-components
  espressif/esp_lcd_co5300 2.2.0, the screen driver
      https://github.com/espressif/esp-iot-solution
  espressif/esp_lcd_touch 1.2.1, espressif/esp_lcd_touch_cst816s 1.1.2 and
  espressif/esp_lcd_touch_ft5x06 1.1.1, the touch drivers
      https://github.com/espressif/esp-bsp
  espressif/esp_lvgl_port 2.9.0, LVGL on ESP-IDF
      https://github.com/espressif/esp-bsp
  espressif/esp_io_expander 1.2.1 and espressif/esp_io_expander_tca9554 2.0.3, the I/O expander
      https://github.com/espressif/esp-bsp

The Espressif components are Copyright Espressif Systems (Shanghai) CO LTD.

The board package also asks for espressif/esp_codec_dev, espressif/esp_lcd_panel_io_additions and
espressif/cmake_utilities (all Apache License 2.0). They are part of the build, but none of their
code ends up in the firmware.

Source: https://github.com/lvgl/lvgl, version 9.6.0 (commit
60b614c23c816ca5edc5f2840c9945eff7da0ad4).

================================================================================================
3. Rust crates
================================================================================================

The programs are built from Rust crates, most of them from crates.io: hub-manager, hub-send,
hub-firmware, display-simulator, usage-helper and usage-helper-sync in the app downloads, and hubd
and hub-send in the Raspberry Pi download. This part lists every crate any of them uses on the
systems the downloads are for, with its licence. Crates used only to build or test Display Hub
aren't included, nor are Display Hub's own.

The crates under the Mozilla Public License 2.0 (MPL-2.0) are dwrote, option-ext and serialport.
They are used unmodified. The MPL asks that their source code stays available: it is on crates.io
(https://crates.io/crates/<name>) and in the repository listed with each crate in the MPL-2.0
section below.

gpui-pre-windows 0.3.11 (Apache License 2.0), GPUI's Windows support, is used in a changed copy:
it compiles the app's Windows shaders when the app first starts, not while it is built. Display
Hub's source code has the copy in vendor/gpui-pre-windows, and vendor/README.md says what changed.

The licences:

  MIT License (MIT)
  Apache License 2.0 (Apache-2.0)
  Unicode License v3 (Unicode-3.0)
  ISC License (ISC)
  BSD 3-Clause "New" or "Revised" License (BSD-3-Clause)
  Mozilla Public License 2.0 (MPL-2.0)
  zlib License (Zlib)
  BSD 2-Clause "Simplified" License (BSD-2-Clause)
  Creative Commons Zero v1.0 Universal (CC0-1.0)

Each licence text below is followed by the crates it covers. Some crates have more than one text,
for example one for each of their files. A crate that offers a choice of licences is listed under
the one Display Hub uses it under.

/// The PUA code points special-cased in the GB18030 encoder.
pub(crate) static GB18030_2022_OVERRIDE_PUA: [u16; 18] = [
    0xE78D, 0xE78E, 0xE78F, 0xE790, 0xE791, 0xE792, 0xE793, 0xE794, 0xE795, 0xE796, 0xE81E, 0xE826,
    0xE82B, 0xE82C, 0xE832, 0xE843, 0xE854, 0xE864,
];

/// The bytes corresponding to the PUA code points special-cased in the GB18030 encoder.
pub(crate) static GB18030_2022_OVERRIDE_BYTES: [[u8; 2]; 18] = [
    [0xA6, 0xD9],
    [0xA6, 0xDA],
    [0xA6, 0xDB],
    [0xA6, 0xDC],
    [0xA6, 0xDD],
    [0xA6, 0xDE],
    [0xA6, 0xDF],
    [0xA6, 0xEC],
    [0xA6, 0xED],
    [0xA6, 0xF3],
    [0xFE, 0x59],
    [0xFE, 0x61],
    [0xFE, 0x66],
    [0xFE, 0x67],
    [0xFE, 0x6D],
    [0xFE, 0x7E],
    [0xFE, 0x90],
    [0xFE, 0xA0],
];

//! EdDSA Signatures.

use super::ops::ELEM_LEN;
use crate::digest;

pub mod signing;
pub mod verification;

/// The length of an Ed25519 public key.
pub const ED25519_PUBLIC_KEY_LEN: usize = ELEM_LEN;

pub fn eddsa_digest(signature_r: &[u8], public_key: &[u8], msg: &[u8]) -> digest::Digest {
    let mut ctx = digest::Context::new(&digest::SHA512);
    ctx.update(signature_r);
    ctx.update(public_key);
    ctx.update(msg);
    ctx.finish()
}

use crate::limb::Limb;

#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
#[repr(transparent)]
pub struct N0([Limb; 2]);

impl N0 {
    #[cfg(feature = "alloc")]
    pub(super) const LIMBS_USED: usize = 64 / crate::limb::LIMB_BITS;

    #[inline]
    pub const fn precalculated(n0: u64) -> Self {
        #[cfg(target_pointer_width = "64")]
        {
            Self([n0, 0])
        }

        #[cfg(target_pointer_width = "32")]
        {
            Self([n0 as Limb, (n0 >> crate::limb::LIMB_BITS) as Limb])
        }
    }
}

use crate::error::{KeyRejected, Unspecified};

impl From<untrusted::EndOfInput> for Unspecified {
    fn from(source: untrusted::EndOfInput) -> Self {
        super::erase(source)
    }
}

impl From<core::array::TryFromSliceError> for Unspecified {
    fn from(source: core::array::TryFromSliceError) -> Self {
        super::erase(source)
    }
}

impl From<KeyRejected> for Unspecified {
    fn from(source: KeyRejected) -> Self {
        super::erase(source)
    }
}

// Copyright 2018 Brian Smith.

//
// THE SOFTWARE IS PROVIDED "AS IS" AND THE AUTHOR DISCLAIMS ALL WARRANTIES

// MERCHANTABILITY AND FITNESS. IN NO EVENT SHALL THE AUTHOR BE LIABLE FOR ANY
// SPECIAL, DIRECT, INDIRECT, OR CONSEQUENTIAL DAMAGES OR ANY DAMAGES
// WHATSOEVER RESULTING FROM LOSS OF USE, DATA OR PROFITS, WHETHER IN AN ACTION
// OF CONTRACT, NEGLIGENCE OR OTHER TORTIOUS ACTION, ARISING OUT OF OR IN
// CONNECTION WITH THE USE OR PERFORMANCE OF THIS SOFTWARE.

#[cfg(target_arch = "x86")]
pub fn shift_full_blocks<const BLOCK_LEN: usize>(
    in_out: super::overlapping::Overlapping<'_, u8>,
    mut transform: impl FnMut(&[u8; BLOCK_LEN]) -> [u8; BLOCK_LEN],
) {
    let (in_out, src) = in_out.into_slice_src_mut();
    let in_out_len = in_out[src.clone()].len();

    for i in (0..in_out_len).step_by(BLOCK_LEN) {
        let block = {
            let input =
                <&[u8; BLOCK_LEN]>::try_from(&in_out[(src.start + i)..][..BLOCK_LEN]).unwrap();
            transform(input)
        };
        let output = <&mut [u8; BLOCK_LEN]>::try_from(&mut in_out[i..][..BLOCK_LEN]).unwrap();
        *output = block;
    }
}

/// Polyfill for `Option::unwrap()` as a const fn; feature `const_option`.
/// https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/67441.
/// TODO(MSRV): Replace this with `x.unwrap()`.
///
/// `T: Copy` avoids "constant functions cannot evaluate destructors."
pub const fn unwrap_const<T>(x: Option<T>) -> T
where
    T: Copy,
{
    if let Some(x) = x {
        x
    } else {
        panic!("unwrap_const on `None`");
    }
}

//! Utilities to make dealing with slices less tediuous.

/// Replaces the first N elements of `a` with the first N elements of `b`, where
/// N is `core::cmp::min(a.len(), b.len())`, leaving the rest unchanged.
pub fn overwrite_at_start<T: Copy>(a: &mut [T], b: &[T]) {
    a.iter_mut().zip(b).for_each(|(a, b)| {
        *a = *b;
    });
}

use crate::testutil;
use core::{marker::PhantomData, mem::size_of};

/// A ZST that can be added to any type to make the type `!Send`.
#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
pub struct NotSend(PhantomData<*mut ()>);

impl NotSend {
    pub const VALUE: Self = Self(PhantomData);
}

#[allow(deprecated)]
const _: () = testutil::compile_time_assert_clone::<NotSend>();
#[allow(deprecated)]
const _: () = testutil::compile_time_assert_copy::<NotSend>();
const _: () = assert!(size_of::<NotSend>() == 0);

// SHALL THE AUTHORS OR COPYRIGHT HOLDERS BE LIABLE FOR ANY

// IN CONNECTION WITH THE SOFTWARE OR THE USE OR OTHER
// DEALINGS IN THE SOFTWARE.

pub fn escape(text: &str) -> String {
    let mut quoted = String::new();
    escape_into(text, &mut quoted);
    quoted
}

fn escape_into(text: &str, buf: &mut String) {
    buf.reserve(text.len());
    for c in text.chars() {
        if is_meta_character(c) {
            buf.push('\\');
        }
        buf.push(c);
    }
}

fn is_meta_character(c: char) -> bool {
    match c {
        '\\' | '.' | '+' | '*' | '?' | '(' | ')' | '|' | '[' | ']' | '{' | '}' | '^' | '$'
        | '#' | '&' | '-' | '~' => true,
        _ => false,
    }
}

#define QOI_IMPLEMENTATION
#include "qoi.h"
#include <stddef.h>
#include <stdint.h>

int LLVMFuzzerTestOneInput(const uint8_t *data, size_t size) {
	int w, h;
	if (size < 4) {
		return 0;
	}

	qoi_desc desc;
	void* decoded = qoi_decode((void*)(data + 4), (int)(size - 4), &desc, *((int *)data));
	if (decoded != NULL) {
		free(decoded);
	}
	return 0;
}
